# Findings register

One row per finding ever raised by any cycle. The source of truth for CLV Check 5. Cycle files hold the *why*; this file holds the *closure*.

### CLV Check 5 — the mechanism (POINTER, not a copy — repaired 2026-08-30)

**The mechanism is `scripts/clv_findings.py`. This section describes it and does not restate it.**

```sh
scripts/clv_findings.py                 # human-readable report; exit 0 = pass, 1 = fail, 2 = bad input
scripts/clv_findings.py --limit 0       # every row of every failing bucket
scripts/clv_findings.py --json          # machine-readable
python3 -m unittest discover -s scripts -p 'test_clv_findings.py'   # its 18 synthetic self-tests
```

**Why this section no longer contains the algorithm.** It used to hold an awk one-liner, and
`scripts/clv-lite.sh` held a hand-made copy of the same one-liner, with a comment in the shell
copy asking whoever edited this block to remember to re-sync the other. Two copies of a predicate
are two predicates, and the gate's verdict depended on which one you happened to run. There is now
exactly one implementation, it lives in `scripts/`, and — closing the "Known residual" this section
used to carry — it has a companion test file the way `ledger_drift_lint.py`/TEST-017 do.

**The five concepts, kept apart on purpose.** Conflating any two of them is how this register
produced a clean gate over a live blocker:

| concept | question it answers | unreadable ⇒ |
|---|---|---|
| malformed row | can the row be read at all? | **FAIL** |
| severity | how bad is it? | **FAIL** |
| disposition | is work still owed? | **FAIL** |
| effect set (`BLOCKS: {…}`) | what does it block? | an INDEPENDENT axis |
| outstanding | blocking severity **and** no closing disposition | **FAIL** |

`OUTSTANDING` is computed from severity and disposition ONLY. **Writing a `BLOCKS: {…}` set onto a
row closes nothing and moves nothing out of `OUTSTANDING`** — an effect set says what a finding
blocks, a disposition says whether anyone still owes work on it, and they are separately true or
false. A gate whose actions cannot change its own pass criteria is not a gate, and one previous
NEXT_GATE was written on the opposite belief.

**What changed, and in which direction.** Every change makes MORE things fail, never fewer:

- *Malformed rows were fail-OPEN.* 28 rows split into the wrong number of cells and were counted
  `NEEDS-CLASSIFICATION` — "reported, not blocking" — while several of them literally begin
  `**BLOCKING — …**`. A 29th was worse: `ORCH-009` had seven columns AND no trailing pipe, which
  splits into exactly the field count a well-formed six-column row produces, so the old predicate
  read it as valid and took its severity from the wrong cell. All 29 are repaired (see ROW
  OVERFLOW below) and an unreadable row is now a hard failure.
- *Prose severities were fail-OPEN.* The old predicate was "if the severity cell does not start
  with `blocking`, skip this row". A cell reading `The three completion slots' TAIL …` does not
  start with `blocking`, so the row left the check entirely. That is not a finding that the row is
  harmless; it is the absence of any finding. Such rows are now `UNKNOWN-SEVERITY` and they fail.
- *Prose dispositions were read by accident*, as open or closed depending only on which word they
  happened to begin with. They are now `UNKNOWN-DISPOSITION` and they fail.
- *Vocabulary*, unchanged from VAL-018's ruling and carried across deliberately: matching is
  case-insensitive on the cell's LEADING token, so `FIXED 2026-08-03 (mitigated per DEC-020…)`
  counts as dispositioned, and `blocking-for-clean-build` / `was-blocking-for-production` count as
  blocking. `fix-now` remains a CLOSURE — its convention here is `fix-now` plus the actual
  resolution in `resolved-by` — because re-reading it as open would silently re-open 36 historical
  rows on a vocabulary opinion rather than on evidence.
- *`NEEDS-DISPOSITION`* (added 2026-08-23 when B-R028-17 exposed the hole: a finding that may well
  be blocking, but whose severity the orchestrator declined to set unilaterally, was invisible)
  now FAILS rather than being reported-and-passed. Declining to classify must be visible, and an
  undetermined severity is an undetermined gate. This also retires ORCH-041, whose defect was that
  the old branch short-circuited before the disposition check so the bucket could never self-clear.

### ROW OVERFLOW — what `**[overflow col N …]**` means

The register schema has SIX columns. Twenty-three rows had been written with a seventh (two with an
eighth and ninth) holding extra narrative. **Every Markdown renderer DROPS a cell beyond the header
count**, so that text was already invisible in any rendered view of this file and unaddressable by
any positional mechanism. Those cells were merged into `resolved-by`, each introduced by
`**[overflow col N — preserved verbatim …]**`. Nothing was deleted, rewritten or summarised: the
repair was verified by a content-stream invariant — every non-whitespace character of the original
row is present in the repaired row — on all 29 rows. Six further rows carried an unescaped `|`
inside a cell (mostly a C++ `||` inside backticks; GFM does not let a code span protect a pipe, so
`\|` is the only escape) and those pipes are now escaped.

**Do not add a seventh column.** Put the narrative in `resolved-by`.

**Current run (2026-09-01, AFTER the A3-R027-F8 closure), over 364 rows:**
**0 MALFORMED · 0 UNKNOWN-SEVERITY · 0 UNKNOWN-DISPOSITION · 0 NEEDS-DISPOSITION · 27 OUTSTANDING
· 337 OK**, and **0 of the 27 outstanding rows carry no effect set** (`MISSING-EFFECT = 0`). (2026-08-30: 34/330.)
Reproduce with `scripts/clv_findings.py`; exit code is 1, because `OUTSTANDING` is non-empty.

**`OUTSTANDING` ROSE 15 → 34, and that is the gate WORKING, not a regression.** It was 15 only
because 114 rows carried a severity no mechanism could read and 8 carried an unreadable
disposition — those rows were unjudged, not harmless. Gate 1A transcribed each cell's severity and
disposition from the sentence in that row's own body that already established it, keeping the
original text after the prefix; nothing was re-adjudicated and no row's meaning was changed. The
arithmetic of the change: −3 (ORACLE-F1, A3-R028c-F1, A3-R028c-F2, adjudicated on re-executed
evidence — see [[ORCH-050]]), −1 (ORCH-050 itself, resolved by that adjudication), +23 rows that
were already blocking and open and are now legible to the mechanism.

**Effect sets are NOT dispositions.** Gate 1B wrote an explicit `BLOCKS: {…}` set onto all 27
outstanding rows that lacked one, read off each row's OWN recorded harm, with scope PROVEN rather
than assumed (`references/orchestration.md`) — so an unproven-scope row names its raising TASK, never
a global block. `OUTSTANDING` stayed at exactly 34 across that edit, which is [[LSN-083]] executed
rather than asserted.

Historical runs, kept for provenance and NOT a current status: the 2026-08-30 gate-repair pass
reported 0 / 114 / 8 / 0 / 15 / 227; 2026-08-24 reported 11 OUTSTANDING / 27 NEEDS-CLASSIFICATION;
2026-08-23 reported 8 / 27 / 1. The last two came from the fail-open predicate and are not
comparable with the ones above.

Second-order: `recordImport` re-resolves the now-EMPTY uid and early-returns, so those downloads are imported but recorded in NO sidecar — dedup silently broken for that account. | **FIXED 2026-08-03 (mitigated per DEC-020 Option C, commit `f001c7d20`)** | **RESOLVED 2026-08-03 by DEC-020 Option C (commit `f001c7d20`)** — `GarminConnect::accountStillConnected()` re-reads the on-disk credential on EVERY call (the same `GarminTokenStore::loadChecked` predicate `open()` gates on, deliberately with NO carve-out for the injected-client test seam or the uid override) and now gates both `readFile` and `readdir`; a permission-rejected token file counts as disconnected, matching REQ-006's forced-SSO behaviour. TEST-057 reproduces the exact exploit (a SECOND instance disconnects while the first stays live) and was RED pre-fix. Orchestrator ran its OWN mutation (`accountStillConnected` -> `return true`) and killed 4 slots, independently confirming the gate is load-bearing. **Residual accepted by DEC-020:** the check is inherently TOCTOU-racy (a disconnect landing between check and download still gets ONE activity through), and the restored garth session stays alive in the worker, so anything holding an `IGarminDownloadClient` directly rather than through `GarminConnect` is still unblocked. Both belong to the DEFERRED lifecycle REQ seeded by F10+F12 — Option C mitigates the reachable UI path, it does not bind session lifetime to the account. Original adjudication follows: Adjudicates B-R012-01 as a REAL defect, NOT a wording problem: the criterion clause "deletes the token file BEFORE clearing in-memory state" is not merely unencodable, it is UNIMPLEMENTED and observably exploitable. **Do not reword the clause away.** Rooted in DEC-019's choice to mint a fresh instance in deleteClicked() precisely to avoid touching live instances — so the fix reopens DEC-019 or raises a follow-on REQ; it is not patchable in design.md. The REQ-012 TEST slice itself is safe to commit (adversary's own assessment). Orchestrator independently confirmed AddCloudWizard.cpp:889-893, ConfigDialog.cpp:37-41 parentless, MainWindow.cpp:2561-2566 leak, CloudService.cpp:1400 readFile, and the absent dialog dtor. NOTE: the adversary's phrasing "no `store->close()` anywhere in CloudService.cpp" is loose — close() IS called at :1836/:1845/:1896, but only in the CloudServiceAutoDownload provider path, never by the sync dialog; the substantive claim stands. |

Adversary reproduced the crash under AddressSanitizer with TWO independent minimal harnesses (raw-QObject, and real QWidget+WA_DeleteOnClose+close() inside a nested QEventLoop) — `heap-use-after-free` on the read immediately after the nested loop returns. **This is exactly the hazard the build briefing warned about** ("getting this wrong turns a leak into a double-free/UAF, strictly worse than the bug we are fixing"); the builder reasoned it safe from Qt's DeferredDelete loop-level semantics and did NOT test it. **NOT a flaw in DEC-021's epoch mechanism** (which survived aggressive mutation) — it is a flaw in REQ-017(e)'s teardown, i.e. the very compensating control DEC-021 leans on. `MainWindow::syncCloud` is UNAFFECTED (stack-allocated, deliberately never sets the attribute — the attribute is even commented out at CloudService.cpp:436 for this reason). | **FIXED 2026-08-05 (DEC-024 Option A, as amended).** The gate lives on `QDialog::done(int)` — the choke point `close()`/`reject()`/`accept()`/Escape all funnel through — plus an RAII `BlockingCall` depth counter around all four nested-loop call sites and a deferred replay once the count reaches 0. **DEC-024 as originally written was INSUFFICIENT and the builder proved it instead of following it:** on Qt 6.8.2 `reject()`/Escape destroy a `WA_DeleteOnClose` dialog even when `closeEvent()` ignores, because `QDialog::closeEvent` routes to `reject()`→`done()`. Orchestrator independently re-ran the decisive mutation: removing the `done()` gate reproduces the ASan `heap-use-after-free`; restore was byte-identical (md5 `0cd1dfb3…`) and 26/26 green. Suite 25/25 Garmin, 26/26 full, app links, ASan target passes. | Superseded candidate list: (A) `closeEvent()` override + a blocking-call-in-flight flag set around each blocking* call, deferring destruction until the frame unwinds; (B) drop `WA_DeleteOnClose` and use the explicit-owner pattern `MainWindow::syncCloud` already proves safe; (C) revert to the leak for this one call site. Whatever is chosen MUST ship with an ASan-backed test — the adversary's own lesson is that Qt lifetime claims must be executed, not reasoned. |

| A3-R017b-F1 | A3-recheck/REQ-017 | **BLOCKING (second route to the SAME use-after-free; also introduced by this changeset)** | **The DEC-024 gate is on the wrong LAYER.** It intercepts close-INITIATION (`closeEvent`/`done`), but Qt destroys child widgets **directly** when their parent is destroyed — no `closeEvent`, no `done`, no virtual dispatch. So `~CloudServiceSyncDialog` still runs `closeAndDeleteStore(store)` while a nested `QEventLoop` is on the stack, freeing the store mid-`readFile`. All four sites orchestrator-verified: the dialog is parented to `context->mainWindow` (CloudService.cpp:709), **`MainWindow` itself carries `setAttribute(Qt::WA_DeleteOnClose)`** (MainWindow.cpp:143), and the destructor (CloudService.cpp:981-987) has **no `blockingCallDepth` check anywhere**. Adversary proved the Qt behaviour with an isolated non-GoldenCheetah repro: with a `QDialog` whose `closeEvent()`/`done()` both refuse, `delete parent` ran ONLY the real destructor — neither override fired. **Exposure is not a lab artifact:** GarminConnect.cpp:41-43 sets 30s restore / 60s download / 60s list timeouts, so the window is up to a minute per call. Trigger: the user closes the athlete's MainWindow mid-sync — which the modeless dialog exists precisely to allow. **Pre-Slice-B there was NO destructor, so parent teardown LEAKED the store rather than freeing it — this changeset introduced this route too.** Widened scope: all ~16 CloudService subclasses run `QEventLoop`s in `open()`/`readdir()` (adversary grepped each), so this is not Garmin-specific. | **FIXED 2026-08-06 (DEC-025, TEST-072/073, ASan-verified in BOTH directions by builder and orchestrator independently) — BLOCKING CLEARED** | Root cause (A3-R017b-F4): the guard is wired to close-initiation, not to the operation that is actually unsafe — deleting the store while `blockingCallDepth > 0`. A destructor cannot be vetoed like a virtual `done()`, so the check must move INTO `~CloudServiceSyncDialog`/`closeAndDeleteStore`: when a call is in flight, decline to delete the store (deliberately leak, which is exactly the pre-REQ-017 behaviour and strictly better than a UAF) or hand it to a deferred reaper. Alternatives: reparent the dialog to `nullptr` for its lifetime, or a MainWindow event filter deferring teardown while a child reports busy. |

Mechanism verified in BOTH directions on an isolated TU: `-std=c++17` leaves an undefined external `QBluetoothUuid::toUInt128`, `-std=gnu++17` resolves inline. |

### REQ-028 Option C disposition override — 2026-08-22  (HISTORICAL NARRATIVE — NO CURRENT AUTHORITY)

**RELABELLED 2026-08-30 by Gate 1A, closing [[ORCH-050]]. This block is NOT a disposition source and
carries no verdict.** It was written as a second disposition source for rows whose own cells said
something else, which is exactly the defect ORCH-050 records: two statements of one finding's
disposition, in one file, disagreeing. Every verdict below has been moved INTO the disposition cell
of the row it is about, with that row's original text preserved verbatim after it, and each move was
made only after the cited evidence was RE-EXECUTED at `37710370e` (TEST-128; TEST-129 across three
completion channels and both drivers; `GC_FUZZ_SEED=447` — all PASS on BOTH `offscreen` and
`minimal`) and the prescribed fixes were located in the shipped source. **Read the row, not this
block.** Kept, unedited below, as dated provenance for how those verdicts were reached:

- **A3-R028c-F1 — RESOLVED.** TEST-128 was RED on the stale completion relabelling the restarted row, then GREEN after operation-keyed records survived Refresh with a null row.
- **A3-R028c-F2 — RESOLVED.** All three completion-tail rows and both parseable-driver rows in TEST-129 were independently RED, then GREEN after batch-generation checks; seed 32 passes on both backends.
- **ORACLE-F1 — RESOLVED.** The common stale-frame generation mechanism closes the held-row route; seed 447 passes on both backends under ASan.
- **A3-R028c-F3/A3-R028c-F8 — RESOLVED.** DEC-036 Option C removed scalar/capped retirement; TEST-126 now directly enforces dispatches = matched completions + outstanding records on every generated run.
- **New A3 attack-the-fix blockers — RESOLVED before acceptance.** SixCycle now reads identity from the actual sender reply; syncNext/uploadNext recheck generation after parseable openRideFile suspension; TEST-126 consumes the diagnostic accessor; the queued test callback no longer captures a dead stack local. Full offscreen is 78/0 and `garmin-fast` 25/0.
- **A3 recheck residual — OPEN, non-blocking.** SixCycle's sender-bound fix is source-reviewed and application-build verified, but no unit test crosses its real two-`QNetworkReply` reversed-completion boundary. Add that provider-specific integration test in the Cloud-adapter hardening slice.
- **A3 boundary residual — OPEN, non-blocking.** The `quint64` allocator skips zero but has no forced-wrap/live-ID collision policy or test. This requires roughly 2^64 allocations while the colliding record remains live and is not release-blocking; define collision retry and a forced-boundary seam before changing allocator semantics.
- **A3 recheck oracle residual — RESOLVED.** TEST-126 prose now describes direct observation and completion helpers resample `outstandingTransferCount()` after production removal; focused offscreen/minimal each pass 6/0.

REQ-028(c) remains partial for the independent sorting/positional-driver slice; this override does not close that defect.

Routing: UNROUTED — raised against REQ-028, which is TEST VERIFIED / COMMITTED; DEC-038 later suspends list sorting, but no row records that as this finding's disposition. | **REASONED FROM CODE, NOT TRACED** — the scout explicitly declined to claim the route, because it did not confirm the column header is user-clickable in this dialog's layout, and per [[LSN-063]] a guard's reachability needs a trace, not an inference. **SHARPENED 2026-08-16 while verifying TEST-107's P1 at Qt's source, and it makes this finding harder rather than easier:** `QTreeWidgetItem::child(int)` (`qtreewidget.h:145-150`) calls **`executePendingSort()`** before returning the item. Qt's sorting is therefore LAZY — a pending re-sort is applied *at the moment of the addressing call itself*. So the reorder does not merely happen "sometime between capture and use": it can be executed **inside the very `child(listindex-1)` expression** the completion slots use, which means no guard placed *around* that expression can observe the list in its pre-sort order. Any Phase-2 design that assumes "check the container, then address it" has a window it cannot close on this route. That refusal is correct and is why this is a finding rather than a REQ. The wider point: [[LSN-060]] said the suspension set must include the ROW, and this shows the enumeration is *still* incomplete — the row can be invalidated by REORDERING, not only by destruction, and every axis on record assumed destruction. |

| S-R028f-F2 | Sweep/F2 sibling-provider boundedness (2026-08-26) | non-blocking | **AN EXTERNAL `QThread::quit()` CANNOT BE USED TO BOUND THE WORKER, BECAUSE IT TURNS AN UNFINISHED REPLY INTO A FALSE SUCCESS AND LEAKS IT.** The orchestrator's own probe (Qt 6.8.2, ASan, exit 0) established that `quit()` interrupts a LOCAL nested `QEventLoop` on a thread whose overridden `run()` never called `QThread::exec()` (199ms vs a 3139ms no-quit control), and that the exit request LATCHES so subsequent local loops return immediately (0ms). **What the probe did NOT establish, and this sweep did:** what the collapsed frames then do. Every unbounded provider follows Strava's shape (`Strava.cpp:116-127`) — `loop.exec();` then `reply->attribute(QNetworkRequest::HttpStatusCodeAttribute)` and `if (reply->error() != 0)`, with **nothing checking that the reply actually finished**. Under a latch-driven early return the reply is still in flight, so `statusCode` is 0 and `error()` is `NoError`: the provider proceeds as though an empty response SUCCEEDED, and the `QNetworkReply` is never deleted. The identical shape appears in Dropbox (`:121`, `:169`), Azum, SportTracks, Xert, Nolio and PolarFlow. | **OPEN — non-blocking characterisation. BLOCKS: {}.** User disposition 2026-08-26, carried directly into F2's decision constraints: **cancellation must set a thread-safe stop request checked only at safe worklist/download boundaries, and must NOT call `QThread::quit()` while a provider may be inside its own `QEventLoop`.** This is the concrete form of the "the inner chains unwind for free" hazard the user flagged: the unwind is prompt, but promptness is bought with silent data corruption. The synchronous fast path is therefore permitted only when `finished()`/`wait(0)` PROVES `run()` has returned, never when a latch merely made the loops return. | Found while establishing whether `quit()` could rescue the bound that [[S-R028f-F1]] destroyed. Notable as a case where an EXECUTED probe answered its own question correctly and was still over-generalised by the orchestrator — the measurement was sound, the inference from it was not. Same family as [[LSN-053]] (a mutation/measurement result means exactly what it observed, and no more). |

The single-shot is armed against a loop that has already exited, so it can never fire usefully; the only exit from `exec()` is `reply::finished()`, which a hung connection never delivers. Contrast `SixCycle.cpp:116-120` and `:242-246`, which arm the same idiom in the CORRECT order and are genuinely bounded. | **OPEN — non-blocking, PRE-EXISTING production defect, unrelated to REQ-028's changeset. | B-R040-01 | Builder/DEC-040 Stage-1 mid-flight review (2026-08-27) | blocking | **`CloudService::nam()` SATISFIES THE LETTER OF THE AFFINITY RULING AND DEFEATS ITS POINT — it ACCOMMODATES an off-affinity first use instead of catching it.** Current bytes: `QNetworkAccessManager *created = new QNetworkAccessManager();` (constructed on the CALLER's thread), then `if (created->thread() != thread()) created->moveToThread(thread());`, then `created->setParent(this);`.
